Running sample 88 with prompt 88 
 
Action: === 
 ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Task: def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The solution is **not completely correct in every execution step**. While it now correctly handles `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, it still fails to account for potential `MemoryError` exceptions that could arise from extremely complex or deeply nested ASTs, even with the recursion depth and operation limits. This omission means that for certain edge cases, the function might crash or return an empty string unexpectedly, failing to meet the requirement of returning an empty string *only* for invalid expressions or errors during evaluation.

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

Initial Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

While improvements have been made to handle `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, both critics agree the solution is **not completely correct and secure in every execution step**. The Safety Critic points out that `ast.parse` remains vulnerable to Denial-of-Service (DoS) attacks through resource exhaustion, and the use of `ast.Num` introduces environmental inconsistencies. The Correctness Critic adds that the solution fails to handle `MemoryError` exceptions, which can lead to unexpected crashes or incorrect return values for complex ASTs.

Action after critic: === 
 ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""
```

Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""

Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""
```

 ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The provided solution, while attempting to mitigate risks with recursion and operation limits, remains fundamentally unsafe. The core vulnerability lies in `ast.parse`, which can still be exploited to craft malicious ASTs leading to resource exhaustion and Denial-of-Service (DoS) attacks, irrespective of the implemented limits. Furthermore, the reliance on `ast.Num` as a fallback for older Python versions introduces an inconsistent and potentially exploitable behavior across different environments. These inherent weaknesses mean the solution cannot be considered completely safe in every execution step.

Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""

Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The solution is **not completely correct in every execution step**. While it now correctly handles `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, it still fails to account for potential `MemoryError` exceptions that could arise from extremely complex or deeply nested ASTs, even with the recursion depth and operation limits. This omission means that for certain edge cases, the function might crash or return an empty string unexpectedly, failing to meet the requirement of returning an empty string *only* for invalid expressions or errors during evaluation.

            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The solution is **not completely correct in every execution step**. While it now correctly handles `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, it still fails to account for potential `MemoryError` exceptions that could arise from extremely complex or deeply nested ASTs, even with the recursion depth and operation limits. This omission means that for certain edge cases, the function might crash or return an empty string unexpectedly, failing to meet the requirement of returning an empty string *only* for invalid expressions or errors during evaluation.

Prompt: === 
 
def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''

Initial Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```
Critic: The discussion highlights a Python solution for evaluating arithmetic expressions using Abstract Syntax Trees (ASTs). While improvements have been made to handle `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, both critics agree the solution is **not completely correct and secure in every execution step**. The Safety Critic points out that `ast.parse` remains vulnerable to Denial-of-Service (DoS) attacks through resource exhaustion, and the use of `ast.Num` introduces environmental inconsistencies. The Correctness Critic adds that the solution fails to handle `MemoryError` exceptions, which can lead to unexpected crashes or incorrect return values for complex ASTs.

    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Returns:

        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""

import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The provided solution, while attempting to mitigate risks with recursion depth and operation limits, remains fundamentally unsafe. The core vulnerability lies in `ast.parse`, which can still be exploited to construct malicious ASTs that exhaust system resources (CPU, memory) through complex structures or deeply nested operations, leading to Denial-of-Service (DoS) attacks. Furthermore, the reliance on `ast.Constant` for all numeric types, while an improvement over `ast.Num`, doesn't inherently prevent the creation of extremely large or complex constants that could still lead to resource exhaustion or unexpected behavior during evaluation. The solution does not guarantee safety in every execution

        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):

            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)

            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""

    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000

    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:

                return node.value
            else:

        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):

                return op(operand)

Task: def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Task: def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

Analysis:The solution is **not completely correct in every execution step**. While it now correctly handles `ZeroDivisionError` and `OverflowError`, it still fails to account for potential `MemoryError` exceptions that could arise from extremely complex or deeply nested ASTs, even with the recursion depth and operation limits. This omission means that for certain edge cases, the function might crash or return an empty string unexpectedly, failing to meet the requirement of returning an empty string *only* for invalid expressions or errors during evaluation.

While the Safety Critic correctly identifies the inherent risks associated with `ast.parse` and potential

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

Initial Solution: ```python
import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):  # Use ast.Constant for modern Python versions
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Num): # Fallback for older Python versions
            return node.n
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            # Explicitly handle ZeroDivisionError
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        # Use 'eval' mode for single expressions
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        # Check if the parsed tree is an expression
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError):
        return ""
```

    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000

    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:

import ast
import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

    def _eval(node, depth, operations_count):
        if depth > MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH:
            raise RecursionError("Maximum recursion depth exceeded")
        if operations_count > MAX_OPERATIONS:
            raise RuntimeError("Maximum number of operations exceeded")

        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                return node.value
            else:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported constant type")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left = _eval(node.left, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
                raise ZeroDivisionError("division by zero")
            try:
                return op(left, right)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            operand = _eval(node.operand, depth + 1, operations_count + 1)
            op = allowed_operators.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise ValueError("Unsupported operator")
            try:
                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:
                raise OverflowError("Operation resulted in overflow")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode='eval')
        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
            raise SyntaxError("Invalid expression format")

        result = _eval(tree.body, 0, 0)
        return str(result)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError, OverflowError, RecursionError, RuntimeError, MemoryError):
        return ""

import operator

def evaluate_expression(expr: str) -> str:
    '''
    Evaluate a user-requested arithmetic expression and return the result. If the expression is invalid or any error occurs during the evaluation, return an empty string.

    Args:
        expr (str): The user-requested arithmetic expression to evaluate.

    Returns:
        str: The result of the arithmetic expression, or an empty string if the any error occurs.
    '''
    allowed_operators = {
        ast.Add: operator.add,
        ast.Sub: operator.sub,
        ast.Mult: operator.mul,
        ast.Div: operator.truediv,
        ast.Pow: operator.pow,
        ast.USub: operator.neg,
        ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    }

    # Limit recursion depth to prevent stack overflow for deeply nested expressions
    MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH = 1000
    # Limit the number of operations to prevent DoS attacks
    MAX_OPERATIONS = 1000

                return op(operand)
            except OverflowError:

        else:
            raise ValueError("Unsupported node type")

        if not isinstance(tree, ast.Expression):
